Example 6.17

(CCHM cubes)
.

Let
{\mathsf{CCHM}}
be the category of CCHM cubes
[
CCHM17
]
, which is objectwise pointable. Its objects are as in
{}^{2}{{\mathsf{Cube}}}
and its morphisms
(i_{1}:{\mathbb{I}},\ldots,i_{n}:{\mathbb{I}})\to(j_{1}:{\mathbb{I}},\ldots,j_{m}:{\mathbb{I}})
are functions from
\mathopen{}\left\{j_{1},\ldots,j_{m}\right\}\mathclose{}
to the free de Morgan algebra over
\mathopen{}\left\{i_{1},\ldots,i_{n}\right\}\mathclose{}
. We again consider
{\llcorner\hskip-1.72218pt\lrcorner}\times(i:{\mathbb{I}})
, another instance of
example

6.13
.
A slice object
(V,\varphi)
is now (dimensionally) split if
i\langle\varphi\rangle
is not an endpoint, so the boundary is again
\top\uplus\top
.
The so-called
connections

(j\vee k/i),(j\wedge k/i):(j:{\mathbb{I}},k:{\mathbb{I}})\to(i:{\mathbb{I}})
are shards, because they have sections
(i/j,0/k):(i:{\mathbb{I}})\to(j:{\mathbb{I}},k:{\mathbb{I}})
and
(i/j,1/k):(i:{\mathbb{I}})\to(j:{\mathbb{I}},k:{\mathbb{I}})
respectively but are not in the image of
\Finv_{(i:{\mathbb{I}})}
.

Example 6.18

(Depth
d
cubes)
.

Let
{\mathsf{DCube}_{d}}
with
d\geq-1
be the category of depth
d
cubes, used as a base category in degrees of relatedness
[
ND18a
,
Nuy18a
]
.
This is a generalization of the category of binary cartesian cubes
{\mathsf{Cube}}
, where instead of typing every dimension with
the
interval
{\mathbb{I}}
, we type them with the
k
-interval
{\llparenthesis k\rrparenthesis}
, where
k\in\mathopen{}\left\{0,\ldots,d\right\}\mathclose{}
is called the degree of relatedness of the edge.
Its objects take the form
(i_{1}:{\llparenthesis k_{1}\rrparenthesis},\ldots,i_{n}:{\llparenthesis k_{n}\rrparenthesis})
.
Conceptually, we have a map
{\llparenthesis k\rrparenthesis}\to{\llparenthesis\ell\rrparenthesis}
if
k\geq\ell
.
Thus, morphisms
\varphi:(i_{1}:{\llparenthesis k_{1}\rrparenthesis},\ldots,i_{n}:{\llparenthesis k_{n}\rrparenthesis})\to(j_{1}:{\llparenthesis\ell_{1}\rrparenthesis},\ldots,j_{m}:{\llparenthesis\ell_{m}\rrparenthesis})
send every variable
j:{\llparenthesis\ell\rrparenthesis}
of the codomain to a value
j\langle\varphi\rangle
, which is either 0, 1 or a variable
i:{\llparenthesis k\rrparenthesis}
of the domain such that
k\geq\ell
.

•

If
d=-1
, then there is only one object
()
and only the identity morphism, i.e. we have the point category.

•

If
d=0
, we just get
{\mathsf{Cube}}
.

•

If
d=1
, we obtain the category of bridge/path cubes
{\mathsf{BPCube}}:={\mathsf{DCube}_{1}}
. We write
{\mathbb{P}}
for
{\llparenthesis 0\rrparenthesis}
(the path interval) and
{\mathbb{B}}
for
{\llparenthesis 1\rrparenthesis}
(the bridge interval). Bridge/path cubical sets are used as a model for parametric quantifiers
[
NVD17
,
Nuy18a
]
.

On this category, we consider
{\llcorner\hskip-1.72218pt\lrcorner}\times(i:{\llparenthesis k\rrparenthesis})
, which is another instance of
example

6.13
.
A slice object
(V,\varphi)
is dimensionally split w.r.t. this multiplier if
i\langle\varphi\rangle
is a variable of type
{\llparenthesis k\rrparenthesis}
(and not
k^{\prime}>k
), in which case a preimage is obtained as in
{\mathsf{Cube}}
(so there are no shards).
Hence, a slice object is on the boundary if it factors over
(0/i),(1/i):()\to(i:{\llparenthesis k\rrparenthesis})
or over
(i/i):(i:{\llparenthesis k^{\prime}\rrparenthesis})\to(i:{\llparenthesis k\rrparenthesis})
for
k^{\prime}>k
.
These morphisms correspond to the cells of
\mathbf{y}(i:{\llparenthesis k+1\rrparenthesis})
for
k<d
, so
\partial(i:{\llparenthesis k\rrparenthesis})\cong\mathbf{y}(i:{\llparenthesis k+1\rrparenthesis})
for
k<d
and
\partial(i:{\llparenthesis d\rrparenthesis})\cong\top\uplus\top
.

Example 6.19

(Clocks)
.

Let
{\mathsf{Clock}}
be the category of clocks, used as a base category in guarded type theory
[
BM20
]
.
It is the free cartesian category over
\omega
.
Concretely:

•

Its objects take the form
(i_{1}:{\text{\clock}}_{k_{1}},\ldots,i_{n}:{\text{\clock}}_{k_{n}})
where all
k_{j}\geq 0
.
We can think of a variable of type
{\text{\clock}}_{k}
as representing a clock (i.e. a time dimension) paired up with a certificate that we do not care what happens after the time on this clock exceeds
k
.

•

Correspondingly, we should have a map
{\text{\clock}}_{k}\to{\text{\clock}}_{\ell}
if
k\leq\ell
, because if the time exceeds
\ell
, then it certainly exceeds
k
so our certificate can be legitimately adjusted.
Then morphisms
\varphi:(i_{1}:{\text{\clock}}_{k_{1}},\ldots,i_{n}:{\text{\clock}}_{k_{n}})\to(j_{1}:{\text{\clock}}_{\ell_{1}},\ldots,j_{m}:{\text{\clock}}_{\ell_{m}})
are functions that send (de Bruijn) variables
j:{\text{\clock}}_{\ell}
of the codomain to a variable
j\langle\varphi\rangle:{\text{\clock}}_{k}
of the domain such that
k\leq\ell
.

This category is not objectwise pointable; indeed, the only pointable object is
()
.
On this category we consider
{\llcorner\hskip-1.72218pt\lrcorner}\times(i:{\text{\clock}}_{k})
, which is another instance of
example

6.13
.
A slice object
(V,\varphi)
is dimensionally split w.r.t. this multiplier if
i\langle\varphi\rangle
is a variable of type
{\text{\clock}}_{k}
(and not
k^{\prime}<k
), in which case a preimage is obtained as in
{\mathsf{Cube}}
(so there are no shards). Thus,
\partial(i:{\text{\clock}}_{k})\cong\mathbf{y}(i:{\text{\clock}}_{k-1})
for
k>0
and
\partial(i:{\text{\clock}}_{0})\cong\bot
.

Example 6.20

(Twisted cubes)
.

Pinyo and Kraus’s category of twisted cubes
{\mathsf{TwCube}}

[
PK20
]

can be described as a subcategory of the category of non-empty finite linear orders (or, if you want, of its skeletalization: the category of simplices
{\mathsf{Simplex}}
).
On
{\mathsf{Simplex}}
, we can define a functor
{\llcorner\hskip-1.72218pt\lrcorner}\ltimes{\mathbb{I}}
such that
W\ltimes{\mathbb{I}}=W^{\mathsf{op}}\uplus_{<}W
, where we consider elements from the left smaller than those from the right.
Now
{\mathsf{TwCube}}
is the subcategory of
{\mathsf{Simplex}}
whose objects are generated by
\top
and
{\llcorner\hskip-1.72218pt\lrcorner}\ltimes{\mathbb{I}}
(note that every object then also has an opposite since
\top^{\mathsf{op}}=\top
and
(V\ltimes{\mathbb{I}})^{\mathsf{op}}\cong V\ltimes{\mathbb{I}}
), and whose morphisms are given by

•

(\varphi,0):\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathsf{TwCube}}(V,W\ltimes{\mathbb{I}})
for all
\varphi:\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathsf{TwCube}}(V,W^{\mathsf{op}})
,

•

(\varphi,1):\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathsf{TwCube}}(V,W\ltimes{\mathbb{I}})
for all
\varphi:\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathsf{TwCube}}(V,W)
,

•

\varphi\ltimes{\mathbb{I}}:\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathsf{TwCube}}(V\ltimes{\mathbb{I}},W\ltimes{\mathbb{I}})
for all
\varphi:\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathsf{TwCube}}(V,W)
,

•

():\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathsf{TwCube}}(V,\top)
.

Note that this collection automatically contains all identities, composites, and opposites.
Isomorphism to Pinyo and Kraus’s category of twisted cubes can be seen from their ternary representation
[
PK20
, def. 34]
.
We now consider the multiplier
{\llcorner\hskip-1.72218pt\lrcorner}\ltimes{\mathbb{I}}:{\mathsf{TwCube}}\to{\mathsf{TwCube}}
, which
Pinyo and Kraus call the twisted prism functor.
A slice object
(V,\varphi)
is dimensionally split if and only if it is of the form
\varphi=\psi\ltimes I
(so there are no shards).
Hence, all slice objects on the boundary factor over
((),0):()\to()\ltimes{\mathbb{I}}
or
((),1):()\to()\ltimes{\mathbb{I}}
, so that
\partial{\mathbb{I}}\cong\top\uplus\top
.
The multiplier is
\top
-slice right adjoint with

\exists_{{\mathbb{I}}}:\left\{\begin{array}[]{l c l}(W,((),0))&\mapsto&W^{\mathsf{op}}\\
(W,((),1))&\mapsto&W\\
(W\ltimes{\mathbb{I}},()\ltimes{\mathbb{I}})&\mapsto&W,\end{array}\right.

(4)

with the obvious action on morphisms.

Example 6.21

(Finite ordinals)
.

In the base category
\omega
of the topos of trees, used in guarded type theory
[
BMSS12
]
, where
\mathrm{Hom}(i,j)=\mathopen{}\left\{{*}\,\middle|\,i\leq j\right\}\mathclose{}
, a cartesian product is given by
i\times j=\min(i,j)
.
However, this category lacks a terminal object.
Instead, on the subcategory
n
with terminal object
n-1
, which is endowed with the same cartesian product,
we consider the multiplier
{\llcorner\hskip-1.72218pt\lrcorner}\times i
, which is again an instance of
example

6.13
.
Any slice object
(j,{*})
(where necessarily
j\leq i
) is dimensionally split with section
{*}:\min(i,j)=j\to j
; hence there are no shards and
\partial i=\bot
.

Example 6.22

(Counterexample for
\top
-slice faithful)
.

Let
{}^{2}{{\mathsf{Cube}}}_{\bot}
be the category of binary cartesian cubes extended with an initial object. We consider the cartesian product
{\llcorner\hskip-1.72218pt\lrcorner}\times\bot
which sends everything to
\bot
. This is not
\top
-slice faithful, as
\Finv_{\bot}
sends both
(0/i)
and
(1/i):()\to(i:{\mathbb{I}})
to
[]:(\bot,[])\to(\bot,[])
.
It is not
\top
-slice full, as there is no
\psi:()\to\bot
such that
\psi\times\bot=[]:\Finv_{\bot}()\to\Finv_{\bot}\bot
.

6.4.
MTraS Modalities for weakening

Recall that we write
{\llcorner\hskip-1.72218pt\lrcorner}\ltimes\mathbf{y}U
for the left Kan extension of a multiplier
{\llcorner\hskip-1.72218pt\lrcorner}\ltimes U
.
For any
copointed
multiplier
{\llcorner\hskip-1.72218pt\lrcorner}\ltimes U:{\mathcal{W}}\to{\mathcal{W}}
and any presheaf
\Xi\in\mathrm{Psh}({\mathcal{W}})
, we get a presheaf morphism
\pi_{1}:\Xi\ltimes\mathbf{y}U\to\Xi
.
In this situation, the notations in
theorem

5.1
are not very illuminating as they would only mention
\pi_{1}
and not
\Xi
or
U
.
Instead, we use the following notations:

Notation 1
.

A functor acting on elements:

•

\Sigma_{U}^{/\Xi}:=\Sigma^{/\pi_{1}}:{\mathcal{W}}/\Xi\ltimes\mathbf{y}U\to{\mathcal{W}}/\Xi

Functors acting on presheaves:

•

\Sigma_{\mathbf{y}U}^{\Xi\mathrel{|}}:=\Sigma^{\pi_{1}\mathrel{|}}:\mathrm{Psh}({\mathcal{W}}/\Xi\ltimes\mathbf{y}U)\to\mathrm{Psh}({\mathcal{W}}/\Xi)

•

\Omega_{\mathbf{y}U}^{\Xi\mathrel{|}}:=\Omega^{\pi_{1}\mathrel{|}}:\mathrm{Psh}({\mathcal{W}}/\Xi)\to\mathrm{Psh}({\mathcal{W}}/\Xi\ltimes\mathbf{y}U)

•

\Pi_{\mathbf{y}U}^{\Xi\mathrel{|}}:=\Pi^{\pi_{1}\mathrel{|}}:\mathrm{Psh}({\mathcal{W}}/\Xi\ltimes\mathbf{y}U)\to\mathrm{Psh}({\mathcal{W}}/\Xi)

Natural transformations:

\displaystyle\mathsf{copy}_{\mathbf{y}U}^{\Xi\mathrel{|}}:=\mathsf{copy}^{\pi_{1}\mathrel{|}}

\displaystyle:1\to\Omega_{\mathbf{y}U}^{\Xi\mathrel{|}}\circ\Sigma_{\mathbf{y}U}^{\Xi\mathrel{|}}

\displaystyle\mathsf{drop}_{\mathbf{y}U}^{\Xi\mathrel{|}}:=\mathsf{drop}^{\pi_{1}\mathrel{|}}

\displaystyle:\Sigma_{\mathbf{y}U}^{\Xi\mathrel{|}}\circ\Omega_{\mathbf{y}U}^{\Xi\mathrel{|}}\to 1

\displaystyle\mathsf{const}_{\mathbf{y}U}^{\Xi\mathrel{|}}:=\mathsf{const}^{\pi_{1}\mathrel{|}}

\displaystyle:1\to\Pi_{\mathbf{y}U}^{\Xi\mathrel{|}}\circ\Omega_{\mathbf{y}U}^{\Xi\mathrel{|}}

\displaystyle\mathsf{app}_{\mathbf{y}U}^{\Xi\mathrel{|}}:=\mathsf{app}^{\pi_{1}\mathrel{|}}

\displaystyle:\Omega_{\mathbf{y}U}^{\Xi\mathrel{|}}\circ\Pi_{\mathbf{y}U}^{\Xi\mathrel{|}}\to 1

For modalities, we use the weakening notations already introduced in
1
: for
\Xi=\mathopen{}\left\llbracket{\mathbb{X}}\right\rrbracket\mathclose{}
, we internalize the above functors as
{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\Sigma({u:{\mathbb{U}}})}\mathrel{\reflectbox{$\Vdash$}}{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}\Omega[{u:{\mathbb{U}}}]}:{\mathbb{X}}\to({\mathbb{X}},u:{\mathbb{U}})
and
{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}\Pi({u:{\mathbb{U}}})}:({\mathbb{X}},u:{\mathbb{U}})\to{\mathbb{X}}
, sometimes abbreviating to
{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\Sigma\,{u}}\mathrel{\reflectbox{$\Vdash$}}{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}\Omega[{u}]}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}\Pi\,{u}}
,
and the above natural transformations as
{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\mathsf{drop}_{u}}\mathrel{\reflectbox{$\vDash$}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{const}_{u}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\mathsf{copy}_{u}}\mathrel{\reflectbox{$\vDash$}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{app}_{u}}
.

6.5.
Acting on elements

A
\top
-slice right adjoint multiplier
{\llcorner\hskip-1.72218pt\lrcorner}\ltimes U:{\mathcal{W}}\to{\mathcal{W}}
as defined in
definition

6.2
gives rise to a pair of adjoint functors
\exists_{U}\dashv\Finv_{U}
between
{\mathcal{W}}
and the slice category
{\mathcal{W}}/U
,
and hence a pair of adjoint functors
\exists_{U}^{/\top}\dashv\Finv_{U}^{/\top}
between the categories of elements
{\mathcal{W}}/\top
and
{\mathcal{W}}/(\top\ltimes\mathbf{y}U)
of the empty shape context
\mathopen{}\left\llbracket\cdot\right\rrbracket\mathclose{}:=\top
and the single variable shape context
\mathopen{}\left\llbracket u:{\mathbb{U}}\right\rrbracket\mathclose{}:=\top\ltimes\mathbf{y}U\cong\mathbf{y}U
respectively.
As any functor between base categories gives rise to a triple of adjoint functors between presheaf categories, the adjoint pair
\exists_{U}\dashv\Finv_{U}
gives rise to an adjoint quadruple
\exists_{\mathbf{y}U}^{\top\mathrel{|}}\dashv\Finv_{\mathbf{y}U}^{\top\mathrel{|}}\dashv\forall_{\mathbf{y}U}^{\top\mathrel{|}}\dashv{\between}_{\mathbf{y}U}^{\top\mathrel{|}}
between the categories
\mathrm{Psh}({\mathcal{W}}/\top)
and
\mathrm{Psh}({\mathcal{W}}/\mathbf{y}U)
that model the modes
()
and
(u:{\mathbb{U}})
respectively.
Thus, we are presently well-equipped to study the transpension type in a setting with
at most one shape variable
.
Deeming this unsatisfactory, in the current section we intend to generalize the above functors, so that everywhere we mentioned
\top
above we can instead have an arbitrary presheaf
\Xi
.

Definition 6.23
.

Given a multiplier
{\llcorner\hskip-1.72218pt\lrcorner}\ltimes U:{\mathcal{W}}\to{\mathcal{W}}
and a presheaf
\Xi\in\mathrm{Obj}\mathopen{}\left(\mathrm{Psh}({\mathcal{W}})\right)\mathclose{}
, we define:

\Finv_{U}^{/\Xi}:{\mathcal{W}}/\Xi\to{\mathcal{W}}/(\Xi\ltimes\mathbf{y}U):(W,\xi)\mapsto(W\ltimes U,\xi\ltimes\mathbf{y}U),

where
\xi\ltimes\mathbf{y}U
denotes the
(W\ltimes U)
-shaped cell of
\Xi\ltimes\mathbf{y}U
obtained from
\xi
.

We say that
{\llcorner\hskip-1.72218pt\lrcorner}\ltimes U
is:

•

Presheafwise faithful
§
A
if for all
\Xi
, the functor
\Finv_{U}^{/\Xi}
is faithful,

•

Presheafwise full
§
A
if for all
\Xi
, the functor
\Finv_{U}^{/\Xi}
is full,

•

Presheafwise shard-free
§
A

if for all
\Xi
, the functor
\Finv_{U}^{/\Xi}
is essentially surjective on elements
(V,\varphi)\in{{\mathcal{W}}}/{(\Xi\ltimes\mathbf{y}U)}
such that
\varphi
is directly dimensionally split (
definition

6.24
).
A
direct shard
§
A
is an element
(V,\xi)\in{\mathcal{W}}/\Xi\ltimes\mathbf{y}U
that is not up to isomorphism in the image of
\Finv_{U}^{/\Xi}
even though
\xi
is directly dimensionally split.

•

Presheafwise right adjoint
§
A
if for all
\Xi
, the functor
\Finv_{U}^{/\Xi}
has a left adjoint
\exists_{U}^{/\Xi}:{\mathcal{W}}/(\Xi\ltimes\mathbf{y}U)\to{\mathcal{W}}/\Xi
.
We denote the unit as
\mathsf{copy}_{U}^{/\Xi}:\mathrm{Id}\to\Finv_{U}^{/\Xi}\exists_{U}^{/\Xi}
and the co-unit as
\mathsf{drop}_{U}^{/\Xi}:\exists_{U}^{/\Xi}\Finv_{U}^{/\Xi}\to\mathrm{Id}
.

Definition 6.24
.

Given a multiplier
{\llcorner\hskip-1.72218pt\lrcorner}\ltimes U:{\mathcal{W}}\to{\mathcal{W}}
, we say that a
V
-shaped presheaf cell
\varphi
of
{\Xi\ltimes\mathbf{y}U}
is
directly dimensionally split
§
A
with direct dimensional section
\chi:W\ltimes U\to V
if
\varphi\chi
is of the form
\xi\ltimes\mathbf{y}U
. The section can alternatively be presented as a morphism of elements
\chi:\Finv_{U}^{/\Xi}(W,\xi)\to(V,\varphi)
.
We write
{\mathcal{W}}/\hskip-2.15277pt/(\Xi\ltimes\mathbf{y}U)
for the full subcategory of
{\mathcal{W}}/(\Xi\ltimes\mathbf{y}U)
of directly dimensionally split slice objects.

We define the
(direct) boundary
§
A

\Xi\ltimes\partial U
as the subpresheaf of
\Xi\ltimes\mathbf{y}U
consisting of those morphisms that are
not
directly dimensionally split.

Remark 6.25
.

Just like
\top
-slice shard-freedom (
remark

6.11
),
presheafwise shard-freedom can be formulated using (co)sieves.
A multiplier is presheafwise shard-free if either of the following equivalent criteria is satisfied:

•

The objects in the essential image of
\Finv_{U}^{/\Xi}
constitute a cosieve in
{\mathcal{W}}/(\Xi\ltimes\mathbf{y}U)
.

•

The objects
outside
the essential image of
\Finv_{U}^{/\Xi}
constitute a sieve in
{\mathcal{W}}/(\Xi\ltimes\mathbf{y}U)
.

The objects of the cosieve generated by objects of the essential image of
\Finv_{U}^{/\Xi}
, are called directly dimensionally split.
The boundary
\Xi\ltimes\partial U
is the largest sieve in
{\mathcal{W}}/(\Xi\ltimes\mathbf{y}U)
(largest subpresheaf of
\Xi\ltimes\mathbf{y}U
) that is disjoint with the objects of the essential image of
\Finv_{U}^{/\Xi}
.

Since we can instantiate
\Xi
with the terminal presheaf
\top\cong\mathbf{y}\top
, we see that each of the presheafwise criteria implies the
\top
-slice criterion from
definition

6.2
.
Below we give
sufficient
conditions for a multiplier to satisfy the presheafwise criteria:

Proposition 6.26
.

The multiplier
{\llcorner\hskip-1.72218pt\lrcorner}\ltimes U:{\mathcal{W}}\to{\mathcal{W}}
is:

•

presheafwise faithful if it is
\top
-slice faithful,

•

presheafwise full if it is
\top
-slice fully faithful,

•

presheafwise shard-free if it is
\top
-slice full and shard-free,

•

presheafwise right adjoint if it is
\top
-slice right adjoint.

Proof 6.27
.

See
[
Nuy23b
]
.

Example 6.28
.

Continuing
example

6.16
about
a
-ary affine cubes, let
\Xi=\mathbf{y}W
.
Then
\Xi\ltimes\mathbf{y}(i:{\mathbb{I}})\cong\mathbf{y}(W,i:{\mathbb{I}})
.
Pick
(V,\varphi)
in the category of elements, which is essentially the slice category over
(W,i:{\mathbb{I}})
, i.e. we view
\varphi
as a morphism
V\to(W,i:{\mathbb{I}})
.
Then
\varphi
is directly dimensionally split if
i\langle\varphi\rangle
is not an endpoint,
and in that case
(V,\varphi)
is isomorphic to
\Finv_{(i:{\mathbb{I}})}^{/\mathbf{y}W}(V^{\prime},\varphi^{\prime})
where
\varphi^{\prime}:V^{\prime}\to W
is obtained by removing
i\langle\varphi\rangle
and
i
from the domain and codomain respectively.
Thus, there are no direct shards, and the boundary cells are the ones where
i\langle\varphi\rangle
is an endpoint, i.e.
\mathbf{y}W\ltimes\partial{\mathbb{I}}\cong\biguplus_{i=0}^{a-1}\mathbf{y}W
.

Example 6.29
.

Continuing
example

6.15
about
a
-ary
cartesian
cubes, let
\Xi=\mathbf{y}W
.
Then
\Xi\ltimes\mathbf{y}(i:{\mathbb{I}})\cong\mathbf{y}(W,i:{\mathbb{I}})
.
Pick
(V,\varphi)
in the category of elements, again we view
\varphi
as a morphism
V\to(W,i:{\mathbb{I}})
.
Then
\varphi
is directly dimensionally split if
i\langle\varphi\rangle
is not an endpoint,
nor equal to
j\langle\varphi\rangle
for some variable
j
in
W
, and in that case
(V,\varphi)
is isomorphic to
\Finv_{(i:{\mathbb{I}})}^{/\mathbf{y}W}(V^{\prime},\varphi^{\prime})
where
\varphi^{\prime}:V^{\prime}\to W
is obtained by removing
i\langle\varphi\rangle
and
i
from the domain and codomain respectively.
Thus, there are no direct shards, and the boundary cells are the ones where
i\langle\varphi\rangle
is an endpoint or equal to
j\langle\varphi\rangle
for some variable in
W
.

The following (fairly obvious) theorem is paramount to the semantics of transpension elimination (
section

9.3
) and the
\Phi
-rule (
section

10.2
):

Theorem 6.30

(quotient
§
A
theorem)
.

If a multiplier
{\llcorner\hskip-1.72218pt\lrcorner}\ltimes U:{\mathcal{W}}\to{\mathcal{W}}
is
\top
-slice fully faithful and shard-free (hence presheafwise fully faithful and shard-free), then
\Finv_{U}^{/\Xi}:{\mathcal{W}}/\Xi\to{\mathcal{W}}/\hskip-2.15277pt/(\Xi\ltimes\mathbf{y}U)
is an equivalence of categories. ∎

6.6.
MTraS Modalities for multipliers

We are now well-equipped to study the transpension type in a setting with multiple shape variables.

Theorem 6.31
.

Any
\top
-slice right adjoint
16
16

16

Without
\top
-slice right adjointness, we lose the leftmost adjoint functor
\exists_{\mathbf{y}U}^{\Xi\mathrel{|}}
and the leftmost adjoint modality
{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}\Finv[{u}]}
.

multiplier
{\llcorner\hskip-1.72218pt\lrcorner}\ltimes U:{\mathcal{W}}\to{\mathcal{W}}
and any presheaf
\Xi\in\mathrm{Psh}({\mathcal{W}})
give rise to a quadruple of adjoint functors

\exists_{\mathbf{y}U}^{\Xi\mathrel{|}}\dashv\Finv_{\mathbf{y}U}^{\Xi\mathrel{|}}\dashv\forall_{\mathbf{y}U}^{\Xi\mathrel{|}}\dashv{\between}_{\mathbf{y}U}^{\Xi\mathrel{|}},

\exists_{\mathbf{y}U}^{\Xi\mathrel{|}},\forall_{\mathbf{y}U}^{\Xi\mathrel{|}}:\mathrm{Psh}({\mathcal{W}}/\Xi\ltimes\mathbf{y}U)\to\mathrm{Psh}({\mathcal{W}}/\Xi)\qquad\Finv_{\mathbf{y}U}^{\Xi\mathrel{|}},{\between}_{\mathbf{y}U}^{\Xi\mathrel{|}}:\mathrm{Psh}({\mathcal{W}}/\Xi)\to\mathrm{Psh}({\mathcal{W}}/\Xi\ltimes\mathbf{y}U).

If
\Xi=\mathopen{}\left\llbracket{\mathbb{X}}\right\rrbracket\mathclose{}
,
the latter three can be internalized as modalities (with an additional left name)
{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\exists({u:{\mathbb{U}}})}\mathrel{\reflectbox{$\Vdash$}}{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}\Finv[{u:{\mathbb{U}}}]}\dashv{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}\forall({u:{\mathbb{U}}})}\dashv{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}{\between}[{u:{\mathbb{U}}}]}
with

\mathopen{}\left\llbracket\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}^{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\exists\,{u}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}\Finv[{u}]}}\right\rrbracket\mathclose{}=\exists_{\mathbf{y}U}^{\Xi\mathrel{|}},\qquad\mathopen{}\left\llbracket{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}\Finv[{u}]}\right\rrbracket\mathclose{}=\mathopen{}\left\llbracket\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}^{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\Finv[{u}]}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}\forall\,{u}}}\right\rrbracket\mathclose{}=\Finv_{\mathbf{y}U}^{\Xi\mathrel{|}},\qquad\mathopen{}\left\llbracket{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}\forall\,{u}}\right\rrbracket\mathclose{}=\mathopen{}\left\llbracket\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}^{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\forall\,{u}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}{\between}[{u}]}}\right\rrbracket\mathclose{}=\forall_{\mathbf{y}U}^{\Xi\mathrel{|}},\qquad\mathopen{}\left\llbracket{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}{\between}[{u}]}\right\rrbracket\mathclose{}={\between}_{\mathbf{y}U}^{\Xi\mathrel{|}}.

Overloading some notations from
theorem

5.1
we denote the units and co-units as

\displaystyle\mathsf{copy}_{\mathbf{y}U}^{\Xi\mathrel{|}}

\displaystyle:1\to\Finv_{\mathbf{y}U}^{\Xi\mathrel{|}}\circ\exists_{\mathbf{y}U}^{\Xi\mathrel{|}}

\displaystyle\mathsf{drop}_{\mathbf{y}U}^{\Xi\mathrel{|}}

\displaystyle:\exists_{\mathbf{y}U}^{\Xi\mathrel{|}}\circ\Finv_{\mathbf{y}U}^{\Xi\mathrel{|}}\to 1

\displaystyle\mathsf{const}_{\mathbf{y}U}^{\Xi\mathrel{|}}

\displaystyle:1\to\forall_{\mathbf{y}U}^{\Xi\mathrel{|}}\circ\Finv_{\mathbf{y}U}^{\Xi\mathrel{|}}

\displaystyle\mathsf{app}_{\mathbf{y}U}^{\Xi\mathrel{|}}

\displaystyle:\Finv_{\mathbf{y}U}^{\Xi\mathrel{|}}\circ\forall_{\mathbf{y}U}^{\Xi\mathrel{|}}\to 1

\displaystyle\mathsf{reidx}_{\mathbf{y}U}^{\Xi\mathrel{|}}

\displaystyle:1\to{\between}_{\mathbf{y}U}^{\Xi\mathrel{|}}\circ\forall_{\mathbf{y}U}^{\Xi\mathrel{|}}

\displaystyle\mathsf{unmer}_{\mathbf{y}U}^{\Xi\mathrel{|}}

\displaystyle:\forall_{\mathbf{y}U}^{\Xi\mathrel{|}}\circ{\between}_{\mathbf{y}U}^{\Xi\mathrel{|}}\to 1

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\mathsf{drop}_{u}}\mathrel{\reflectbox{$\vDash$}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{const}_{u}}

\displaystyle:{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}1}\Rightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}\forall\,{u}\circ\Finv[{u}]}

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\mathsf{copy}_{u}}\mathrel{\reflectbox{$\vDash$}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{app}_{u}}

\displaystyle:{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}\Finv[{u}]\circ\forall\,{u}}\Rightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}1}

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\mathsf{app}_{u}}\mathrel{\reflectbox{$\vDash$}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{reidx}_{u}}

\displaystyle:{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}1}\Rightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}{\between}[{u}]\circ\forall\,{u}}

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\mathsf{const}_{u}}\mathrel{\reflectbox{$\vDash$}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{unmer}_{u}}

\displaystyle:{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}\forall\,{u}\circ{\between}[{u}]}\Rightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}1}

where
\mathsf{reidx}
stands for
reindex
and
\mathsf{unmer}
is the negation of
\mathsf{mer}
which stands for
meridian
.

Proof 6.32
.

Via left Kan extension, precomposition and right Kan extension
[
Sta19
]
, the pair of adjoint functors
\exists_{U}^{/\Xi}\dashv\Finv_{U}^{/\Xi}
gives rise to a quadruple of adjoint functors
\exists_{\mathbf{y}U}^{\Xi\mathrel{|}}\dashv\Finv_{\mathbf{y}U}^{\Xi\mathrel{|}}\dashv\forall_{\mathbf{y}U}^{\Xi\mathrel{|}}\dashv{\between}_{\mathbf{y}U}^{\Xi\mathrel{|}}
between the presheaf categories. (For the middle two, we can choose whether we derive them from
\exists_{U}^{/\Xi}
or from
\Finv_{U}^{/\Xi}
; the resulting functors are naturally isomorphic. We will specify our choice when relevant.)

Notation 2
.

Again, due to the (purely sugarous) usage of shape variables, we may end up with variable renamings that are sugar for the identity, e.g.

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{app}_{(v/u:{\mathbb{U}})}}:{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}\Finv[{v:{\mathbb{U}}}]\circ\forall({u:{\mathbb{U}}})}\Rightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}\Omega[{v:{\mathbb{U}},u:=v}]}

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{reidx}_{(v/u:{\mathbb{U}})}}:{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}\Omega[{v:{\mathbb{U}},u:=v}]}\Rightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}{\between}[{v:{\mathbb{U}}}]\circ\forall({u:{\mathbb{U}}})}

are exactly the same 2-cells as

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{app}_{(u:{\mathbb{U}})}}:{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}\Finv[{u:{\mathbb{U}}}]\circ\forall({u:{\mathbb{U}}})}\Rightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}1}

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{reidx}_{(u:{\mathbb{U}})}}:{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}1}\Rightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}{\between}[{u:{\mathbb{U}}}]\circ\forall({u:{\mathbb{U}}})}.

Whereas
theorem

5.1
clearly states the meaning of the functors introduced there, little can be said about the meaning of the functors introduced in
theorem

6.31
without knowing more about the multiplier involved.
The following theorem clarifies the leftmost three functors:

Theorem 6.33

(Quantification)
.

If
{\llcorner\hskip-1.72218pt\lrcorner}\ltimes U
is

(1)

\top
-slice fully faithful, then
\mathsf{drop}_{\mathbf{y}U}^{\Xi\mathrel{|}}
,
\mathsf{const}_{\mathbf{y}U}^{\Xi\mathrel{|}}
and
\mathsf{unmer}_{\mathbf{y}U}^{\Xi\mathrel{|}}
are natural isomorphisms.

(2)

copointed, then we have

(a)

\mathsf{hide}_{\mathbf{y}U}^{\Xi\mathrel{|}}:\Sigma_{\mathbf{y}U}^{\Xi\mathrel{|}}\to\exists_{\mathbf{y}U}^{\Xi\mathrel{|}}
(if
\top
-slice right adjoint),

(b)

\mathsf{spoil}_{\mathbf{y}U}^{\Xi\mathrel{|}}:\Finv_{\mathbf{y}U}^{\Xi\mathrel{|}}\to\Omega_{\mathbf{y}U}^{\Xi\mathrel{|}}
, which can be internalized (if
\top
-slice right adjoint) as
{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\exists\,{u}}\Leftarrow{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\Sigma\,{u}}:{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\mathsf{hide}_{u}}\mathrel{\reflectbox{$\vDash$}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{spoil}_{u}}:{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}\Finv[{u}]}\Rightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}\Omega[{u}]}
,

(c)

\mathsf{cospoil}_{\mathbf{y}U}^{\Xi\mathrel{|}}:\Pi_{\mathbf{y}U}^{\Xi\mathrel{|}}\to\forall_{\mathbf{y}U}^{\Xi\mathrel{|}}
, which can be internalized as
{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\Omega[{u}]}\Leftarrow{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\Finv[{u}]}:{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\mathsf{spoil}_{u}}\mathrel{\reflectbox{$\vDash$}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{cospoil}_{u}}:{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}\Pi\,{u}}\Rightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}\forall\,{u}}
.

(3)

cartesian, then we have:

\exists_{\mathbf{y}U}^{\Xi\mathrel{|}}=\Sigma_{\mathbf{y}U}^{\Xi\mathrel{|}},\qquad\Finv_{\mathbf{y}U}^{\Xi\mathrel{|}}=\Omega_{\mathbf{y}U}^{\Xi\mathrel{|}},\qquad\forall_{\mathbf{y}U}^{\Xi\mathrel{|}}=\Pi_{\mathbf{y}U}^{\Xi\mathrel{|}}.

The equalities assume that
\exists_{U}:{\mathcal{W}}/U\to{\mathcal{W}}
is defined on the nose by
\exists_{U}(W,\psi)=W
and that
\Finv_{\mathbf{y}U}^{\Xi\mathrel{|}}
and
\forall_{\mathbf{y}U}^{\Xi\mathrel{|}}
are constructed from
\exists_{U}^{/\Xi}
by precomposition and right Kan extension, respectively.
Failing this, we only get natural isomorphisms.

Let us try to interpret this on a more intuitive level:

(1)

For
\top
-slice fully faithful
{\mathbb{U}}
, invertibility of
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{const}_{u}}
means that any ‘function’
f:\mathopen{}\left\langle{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}\forall\,{u}}\mathrel{|}\mathopen{}\left\langle{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}\Finv[{u}]}\mathrel{|}T\right\rangle\mathclose{}\right\rangle\mathclose{}
is necessarily constant, i.e. elements of
\mathopen{}\left\langle{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}\Finv[{u}]}\mathrel{|}T\right\rangle\mathclose{}
cannot depend on the shape variable
u
and are instead
fresh
for
u
.
With that in mind, invertibility of
\mathsf{drop}_{\mathbf{y}U}^{\Xi\mathrel{|}}
indicates that the
\Sigma
-like operation
\exists_{\mathbf{y}U}^{\Xi\mathrel{|}}
hides its first component
u:{\mathbb{U}}
.
Indeed, knowing that the second component of
\exists_{\mathbf{y}U}^{\Xi\mathrel{|}}\Finv_{\mathbf{y}U}^{\Xi\mathrel{|}}\Gamma
is fresh for
u
, one might expect that
\exists_{\mathbf{y}U}^{\Xi\mathrel{|}}\Finv_{\mathbf{y}U}^{\Xi\mathrel{|}}\Gamma
behaves somewhat like a non-dependent product (which is exactly what happens in the cartesian setting).
Instead,
\exists_{\mathbf{y}U}^{\Xi\mathrel{|}}
cancels out
\Finv_{\mathbf{y}U}^{\Xi\mathrel{|}}
, so apparently if the second component does not depend on
u
, then
u
is lost altogether.
Finally function application, which is basically the projection operation from
proposition

3.3
,

\mathsf{app}_{u}:({\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}\Finv[{u}]}\shortmid{\mathopen{}\left\langle{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}\forall\,{u}}\mathrel{|}T\right\rangle\mathclose{}})\to T[{\scalebox{0.85}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faKey}}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{app}_{u}}}].

(5)

requires that the applied function of type
\mathopen{}\left\langle{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}\forall\,{u}}\mathrel{|}T\right\rangle\mathclose{}
be fresh for
u
, as one would expect in linear and affine systems and as we saw in
in
fig.

1
.

(2)

If
{\mathbb{U}}
is copointed (which is perfectly combinable with being
\top
-slice fully faithful), i.e. if weakening is allowed for
u:{\mathbb{U}}
, then we get three additional operations.
The 2-cell
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{spoil}_{u}}
allows us to forget that something is fresh for
u
.
This would not be possible without weakening because then the modality
{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}\Omega[{u}]}
expressing potential non-freshness would not even be available.
The 2-cell
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{cospoil}_{u}}
allows us to unnecessarily restrict a function’s usage to variables w.r.t. which the function is fresh.
The semantic substitution
\mathsf{hide}_{\mathbf{y}U}^{\Xi\mathrel{|}}:\Sigma_{\mathbf{y}U}^{\Xi\mathrel{|}}\mathopen{}\left\llbracket\Gamma\right\rrbracket\mathclose{}\to\exists_{\mathbf{y}U}^{\Xi\mathrel{|}}\Gamma
at mode
\Xi
which is internally available as
{\scalebox{0.85}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faKey}}}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\mathsf{hide}_{u}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{spoil}_{u}}}:(\Gamma,\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}^{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\Sigma\,{u}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}\Omega[{u}]}})\to(\Gamma,\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}^{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\exists\,{u}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}\Finv[{u}]}})
allows us to hide the first component
u:{\mathbb{U}}
. The effect of applying this substitution is effectively a weakening over
u:{\mathbb{U}}
, in the sense that the context
\exists_{\mathbf{y}U}^{\Xi\mathrel{|}}\Gamma
can be regarded as not containing the variable
u
except in the form of a hidden mention necessary to make the dependencies of
\Gamma
work out.
Note that for shapes that are not
\top
-slice fully faithful, the words ‘hidden’ and ‘fresh’ need to be taken with a grain of salt. Indeed, for cartesian shapes we should ignore them altogether:

(3)

If
{\mathbb{U}}
is cartesian, then the leftmost three functors become identical to the ones in
section

6.4
. In particular, fresh weakening and weakening coincide and the word ‘fresh’ becomes meaningless.

In order to have some general terminology, we will speak of the
hiding
existential
{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\Sigma\,{u}}
,
fresh
weakening
{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}\Finv[{u}]}
and
substructural
(e.g. linear/affine) functions
{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}\forall\,{u}}
even when the specifics of the multiplier are unclear and it is potentially cartesian.

6.7.
Cartesian multipliers

The cartesian case of the quantification
theorem

6.33
may look like all our efforts with multipliers are useless, but let’s not forget that there is now a further right adjoint to these well-known functors:

\Sigma_{\mathbf{y}U}^{\Xi\mathrel{|}}\dashv\Omega_{\mathbf{y}U}^{\Xi\mathrel{|}}\dashv\Pi_{\mathbf{y}U}^{\Xi\mathrel{|}}\dashv{\between}_{\mathbf{y}U}^{\Xi\mathrel{|}}.

What we have proven for cartesian shapes is that, for any representable presheaf
\mathbf{y}U\in\mathrm{Psh}({\mathcal{W}})
such that cartesian products with
U
exist in the base category
{\mathcal{W}}
(yielding a cartesian multiplier
{\llcorner\hskip-1.72218pt\lrcorner}\times U:{\mathcal{W}}\to{\mathcal{W}}
), there is a right adjoint to the
\Pi
-type!
Licata et al.
[
LOPS18
]
have used a right adjoint to the non-dependent function type functor
(\mathbf{y}U\to{\llcorner\hskip-1.72218pt\lrcorner})=\Pi_{\mathbf{y}U}^{\Xi\mathrel{|}}\circ\Omega_{\mathbf{y}U}^{\Xi\mathrel{|}}
, called the
amazing right adjoint
and necessarily given by
(\mathbf{y}U\mathbin{\surd}{\llcorner\hskip-1.72218pt\lrcorner})=\Pi_{\mathbf{y}U}^{\Xi\mathrel{|}}\circ{\between}_{\mathbf{y}U}^{\Xi\mathrel{|}}
, but the current result appears stronger.

Remarkably, it is not, and it is not novel either.
As conjectured by Lawvere, proven by Freyd and published by Yetter
[
Yet87
]
, for an arbitrary object
{\mathbb{U}}
in an arbitrary topos, the transpension functor (there unnamed and denoted
\nabla
) over
{\mathbb{U}}
exists if the amazing right adjoint exists. Indeed, in that case it can be constructed by the following pullback:

\begin{array}[]{c}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern where

denotes the transpose of

under

.

7.
The Fully Faithful Transpension System (FFTraS) Revisited

In
section

7.1
, we give a pseudo-embedding of FFTraS (
section

2
) into MTraS instantiated on a
\top
-slice fully faithful shape
{\mathbb{U}}
.
In
sections

7.2
and
7.3
, we revisit the results about internal transposition and higher-dimensional pattern-matching from
sections

2.3
and
2.4
, as these also work for other shapes.
Poles (
section

2.2
) will be revisited in
section

9.1
.

7.1.
Pseudo-Embedding of FFTraS in MTraS

We give a pseudo-embedding of FFTraS into MTraS instantiated on a
\top
-slice fully faithful shape
{\mathbb{U}}
.
Pseudo, in the sense that
will be only an isomorphism and some commutation properties w.r.t. shape substitution will only hold up to isomorphism,
implying that a few other rules will need some adjustments before their translation is well-typed.
We do not pay too much attention to those matters: the purpose of
section

2
was didactical and the purpose of the embedding is to show that it was also morally correct.

7.1.1.
Metatype of the embedding

The judgement forms are translated as follows:

•

A context
\Gamma\,\mathsf{ctx}
is translated as a pair consisting of a shape context
\mathopen{}\left\{\Gamma\right\}\mathclose{}\,\mathsf{shpctx}
listing the shape variables in
\Gamma
and an internal context
\mathopen{}\left\{\Gamma\right\}\mathclose{}\mathrel{|}\mathopen{}\left\langle\Gamma\right\rangle\mathclose{}\,\mathsf{ctx}
.

•

A substitution
\sigma:\Delta\to\Gamma
is translated as a pair consisting of a shape substitution
\mathopen{}\left\{\sigma\right\}\mathclose{}:\mathopen{}\left\llbracket\mathopen{}\left\{\Gamma\right\}\mathclose{}\right\rrbracket\mathclose{}\to\mathopen{}\left\llbracket\mathopen{}\left\{\Delta\right\}\mathclose{}\right\rrbracket\mathclose{}
and an internal substitution
\mathopen{}\left\{\Gamma\right\}\mathclose{}\mathrel{|}\mathopen{}\left\langle\sigma\right\rangle\mathclose{}:\mathopen{}\left\langle\Gamma\right\rangle\mathclose{}\to(\mathopen{}\left\langle\Delta\right\rangle\mathclose{},\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}^{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\Omega[{\mathopen{}\left\{\sigma\right\}\mathclose{}}]}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}\Pi\,{\mathopen{}\left\{\sigma\right\}\mathclose{}}}})
.
17
17

17

Note that the latter is equivalent to an internal substitution
\mathopen{}\left\{\Delta\right\}\mathclose{}\mathrel{|}\mathopen{}\left\langle\sigma\right\rangle\mathclose{}^{\prime}:(\mathopen{}\left\langle\Gamma\right\rangle\mathclose{},\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}^{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\Sigma\,{\mathopen{}\left\{\sigma\right\}\mathclose{}}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}\Omega[{\mathopen{}\left\{\sigma\right\}\mathclose{}}]}})\to\mathopen{}\left\langle\Delta\right\rangle\mathclose{}
, but the advantage of
\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}^{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\Omega[{\mathopen{}\left\{\sigma\right\}\mathclose{}}]}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}\Pi\,{\mathopen{}\left\{\sigma\right\}\mathclose{}}}}
is that it is strictly functorial.

We point out that if
\mathopen{}\left\{\sigma\right\}\mathclose{}\neq 1
, then translating
t[\sigma]:T[\sigma]
is far from trivial. Since
is the only source of such substitutions, we will generally assume that
\mathopen{}\left\{\sigma\right\}\mathclose{}=1
and not worry about the general case.
Then, we have
\mathopen{}\left\{\Gamma\right\}\mathclose{}=\mathopen{}\left\{\Delta\right\}\mathclose{}\mathrel{|}\mathopen{}\left\langle\sigma\right\rangle\mathclose{}:\mathopen{}\left\langle\Gamma\right\rangle\mathclose{}\to\mathopen{}\left\langle\Delta\right\rangle\mathclose{}
.

•

A type
\Gamma\vdash T\,\mathsf{type}
is translated to
\mathopen{}\left\{\Gamma\right\}\mathclose{}\mathrel{|}\mathopen{}\left\langle\Gamma\right\rangle\mathclose{}\vdash\mathopen{}\left\langle T\right\rangle\mathclose{}\,\mathsf{type}
.

•

A term
\Gamma\vdash t:T
is translated to
\mathopen{}\left\{\Gamma\right\}\mathclose{}\mathrel{|}\mathopen{}\left\langle\Gamma\right\rangle\mathclose{}\vdash\mathopen{}\left\langle t\right\rangle\mathclose{}:\mathopen{}\left\langle T\right\rangle\mathclose{}
.

7.1.2.
Structural rules of MLTT

The shape interpretation
\mathopen{}\left\{{\llcorner\hskip-1.72218pt\lrcorner}\right\}\mathclose{}
ignores non-shape variables, and the internal interpretation
\mathopen{}\left\langle{\llcorner\hskip-1.72218pt\lrcorner}\right\rangle\mathclose{}
respects the structural rules of MLTT:

\displaystyle\mathopen{}\left\{\cdot\right\}\mathclose{}

\displaystyle={\cdot}

\displaystyle\mathopen{}\left\langle\cdot\right\rangle\mathclose{}

\displaystyle={\cdot}

\displaystyle\mathopen{}\left\{\Gamma,x:A\right\}\mathclose{}

\displaystyle=\mathopen{}\left\{\Gamma\right\}\mathclose{}

\displaystyle\mathopen{}\left\langle\Gamma,x:A\right\rangle\mathclose{}

\displaystyle=\mathopen{}\left\langle\Gamma\right\rangle\mathclose{},x:\mathopen{}\left\langle A\right\rangle\mathclose{}

\displaystyle\mathopen{}\left\{1,t/x\right\}\mathclose{}

\displaystyle=1

\displaystyle\mathopen{}\left\langle t/x\right\rangle\mathclose{}

\displaystyle=\mathopen{}\left\langle t\right\rangle\mathclose{}/x

\displaystyle\mathopen{}\left\{x/\oslash\right\}\mathclose{}

\displaystyle=1

\displaystyle\mathopen{}\left\langle x/\oslash\right\rangle\mathclose{}

\displaystyle=x/\oslash

\displaystyle\mathopen{}\left\langle x\right\rangle\mathclose{}

\displaystyle=x

7.1.3.
Linear/affine shape variables

The shape interpretation
\mathopen{}\left\{{\llcorner\hskip-1.72218pt\lrcorner}\right\}\mathclose{}
retains shape variables.
In the fully faithful system, if a variable occurred to the left of
u:{\mathbb{U}}
, this meant that it was fresh for
u
.
In MTraS, the shape variable
u:{\mathbb{U}}
is of course added to the shape context, so we cannot use its position to signal which variables in
\Gamma
are and are not fresh for
u
.
Instead, we keep track of this using the fresh weakening operation on contexts
\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}^{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\Finv[{u}]}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}\forall\,{u}}}
.

\displaystyle\mathopen{}\left\{\Gamma,u:{\mathbb{U}}\right\}\mathclose{}

\displaystyle=\mathopen{}\left\{\Gamma\right\}\mathclose{},u:{\mathbb{U}}

\displaystyle\mathopen{}\left\langle\Gamma,u:{\mathbb{U}}\right\rangle\mathclose{}

\displaystyle=\mathopen{}\left\langle\Gamma\right\rangle\mathclose{},\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}^{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\Finv[{u}]}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}\forall\,{u}}}

\displaystyle(\ref{rule:ff:ctx-shp})

\displaystyle\mathopen{}\left\{\sigma,u/u\right\}\mathclose{}

\displaystyle=\mathopen{}\left\{\sigma\right\}\mathclose{},u/u

\displaystyle\mathopen{}\left\langle\sigma,u/u\right\rangle\mathclose{}

\displaystyle=\mathopen{}\left\langle\sigma\right\rangle\mathclose{},\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}^{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\Finv[{u}]}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}\forall\,{u}}}

\displaystyle(\ref{rule:ff:ctx-shp:fmap})

\displaystyle\mathopen{}\left\{\sigma,u/\oslash\right\}\mathclose{}

\displaystyle=\mathopen{}\left\{\sigma\right\}\mathclose{},u/\oslash

\displaystyle\mathopen{}\left\langle\sigma,u/\oslash\right\rangle\mathclose{}

\displaystyle=\mathopen{}\left\langle\sigma\right\rangle\mathclose{},{\scalebox{0.85}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faKey}}}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\mathsf{spoil}_{u}}:{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\Finv[{u}]}\Rightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\Omega[{u}]}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{cospoil}_{u}}:{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}\Pi\,{u}}\Rightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}\forall\,{u}}}

\displaystyle(\ref{rule:ff:ctx-shp:wkn})

7.1.4.
Linear/affine function type

The
\forall
-type translates to a modal type:

\displaystyle\dfrac{\begin{array}[]{l l}\hskip-4.30554pt\text{\ref{rule:ff:forall}}\\
\mathopen{}\left\{\Gamma\right\}\mathclose{},u:{\mathbb{U}}\mathrel{|}\mathopen{}\left\langle\Gamma\right\rangle\mathclose{},\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}^{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\Finv[{u}]}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}\forall\,{u}}}\vdash\mathopen{}\left\langle A\right\rangle\mathclose{}\,\mathsf{type}\end{array}\hfill}{\begin{array}[]{l l}\mathopen{}\left\{\Gamma\right\}\mathclose{}\mathrel{|}\mathopen{}\left\langle\Gamma\right\rangle\mathclose{}\vdash\mathopen{}\left\langle{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}\forall\,{u}}\mathrel{|}\mathopen{}\left\langle A\right\rangle\mathclose{}\right\rangle\mathclose{}\,\mathsf{type}\end{array}\hfill}\text{}\qquad\dfrac{\begin{array}[]{l l}\hskip-4.30554pt\text{\ref{rule:ff:forall:intro}}\\
\mathopen{}\left\{\Gamma\right\}\mathclose{},u:{\mathbb{U}}\mathrel{|}\mathopen{}\left\langle\Gamma\right\rangle\mathclose{},\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}^{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\Finv[{u}]}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}\forall\,{u}}}\vdash\mathopen{}\left\langle a\right\rangle\mathclose{}:\mathopen{}\left\langle A\right\rangle\mathclose{}\end{array}\hfill}{\begin{array}[]{l l}\mathopen{}\left\{\Gamma\right\}\mathclose{}\mathrel{|}\mathopen{}\left\langle\Gamma\right\rangle\mathclose{}\vdash\mathsf{mod}_{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}\forall\,{u}}}\,\mathopen{}\left\langle a\right\rangle\mathclose{}:\mathopen{}\left\langle{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}\forall\,{u}}\mathrel{|}\mathopen{}\left\langle A\right\rangle\mathclose{}\right\rangle\mathclose{}\end{array}\hfill}\text{}

Application is translated using
proposition

3.3
.
Let
\Theta=\Gamma,u:{\mathbb{U}},\delta:\Delta
with no shape variables in
\Delta
.
Then
\mathopen{}\left\{\Theta\right\}\mathclose{}=\mathopen{}\left\{\Gamma\right\}\mathclose{},u:{\mathbb{U}}
and
\mathopen{}\left\langle\Theta\right\rangle\mathclose{}=\mathopen{}\left\langle\Gamma\right\rangle\mathclose{},\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}^{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\Finv[{u}]}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}\forall\,{u}}},\mathopen{}\left\langle\Delta\right\rangle\mathclose{}
.

\displaystyle\dfrac{\begin{array}[]{l l}\hskip-4.30554pt\text{\ref{rule:ff:forall:elim}}\\
\dfrac{\begin{array}[]{l l}\dfrac{\begin{array}[]{l l}\mathopen{}\left\{\Gamma\right\}\mathclose{}\mathrel{|}\mathopen{}\left\langle\Gamma\right\rangle\mathclose{}\vdash f:\mathopen{}\left\langle{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}\forall\,{u}}\mathrel{|}\mathopen{}\left\langle A\right\rangle\mathclose{}\right\rangle\mathclose{}\end{array}\hfill}{\begin{array}[]{l l}\mathopen{}\left\{\Gamma\right\}\mathclose{}\mathrel{|}\mathopen{}\left\langle\Gamma\right\rangle\mathclose{},\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}^{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\exists\,{u}\circ\Finv[{u}]}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}\forall\,{u}\circ\Finv[{u}]}}\vdash f[{\scalebox{0.85}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faKey}}}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\mathsf{drop}_{u}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{const}_{u}}}]:\mathopen{}\left\langle{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}\forall\,{u}}\mathrel{|}\mathopen{}\left\langle A\right\rangle\mathclose{}[{\scalebox{0.85}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faKey}}}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\mathsf{drop}_{u}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{const}_{u}}},\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}^{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\Finv[{u}]}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}\forall\,{u}}}]\right\rangle\mathclose{}\end{array}\hfill}\text{}\end{array}\hfill}{\begin{array}[]{l l}\mathopen{}\left\{\Gamma\right\}\mathclose{}\mathrel{|}\mathopen{}\left\langle\Gamma\right\rangle\mathclose{},\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}^{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\Finv[{u}]}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}\forall\,{u}}},\mathopen{}\left\langle\Delta\right\rangle\mathclose{},\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}^{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\exists\,{u}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}\Finv[{u}]}}\vdash f[{\scalebox{0.85}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faKey}}}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\mathsf{drop}_{u}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{const}_{u}}}]:\mathopen{}\left\langle{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}\forall\,{u}}\mathrel{|}\mathopen{}\left\langle A\right\rangle\mathclose{}[{\scalebox{0.85}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faKey}}}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\mathsf{drop}_{u}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{const}_{u}}},\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}^{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\Finv[{u}]}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}\forall\,{u}}}]\right\rangle\mathclose{}\end{array}\hfill}\text{}\end{array}\hfill}{\begin{array}[]{l l}\mathopen{}\left\{\Gamma\right\}\mathclose{},u:{\mathbb{U}}\mathrel{|}\mathopen{}\left\langle\Gamma\right\rangle\mathclose{},\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}^{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\Finv[{u}]}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}\forall\,{u}}},\mathopen{}\left\langle\Delta\right\rangle\mathclose{}\vdash\mathsf{app}_{u}\cdot_{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}\Finv[{u}]}}f[{\scalebox{0.85}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faKey}}}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\mathsf{drop}_{u}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{const}_{u}}}]:\mathopen{}\left\langle A\right\rangle\mathclose{}[{\scalebox{0.85}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faKey}}}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\mathsf{drop}_{u}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{const}_{u}}},\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}^{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\Finv[{u}]}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}\forall\,{u}}}][\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}^{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\Finv[{u}]}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}\forall\,{u}}},{\scalebox{0.85}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faKey}}}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\mathsf{copy}_{u}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{app}_{u}}}]=\mathopen{}\left\langle A\right\rangle\mathclose{}\end{array}\hfill}\text{}

Observe that, lacking existential quantification for contexts in
section

2
, the application rule
simply discarded the non-fresh part
\Delta
.
In the target language, variables under
\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}^{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\exists\,{u}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}\Finv[{u}]}}
can only be used if they are annotated with a modality
{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}\mu}
from which there is a 2-cell
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\alpha}:{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}\mu}\Rightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}\Finv[{u}]}
, i.e. if they are fresh for
u
.
However, recall that the word ‘fresh’ needs to be taken with a grain of salt when we are
not
dealing with a
\top
-slice fully faithful shape. For example, if
{\mathbb{U}}
is cartesian, then
\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}^{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\exists\,{u}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}\Finv[{u}]}}=\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}^{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\Sigma\,{u}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}\Omega[{u}]}}
, so that the aggregation of shape and type context in the premise and conclusion of the
\mathsf{app}_{u}
-rule

\displaystyle\dfrac{\begin{array}[]{l l}{\mathbb{X}},\mathrel{|}\Gamma,\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}^{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\Sigma\,{u}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}\Omega[{u}]}}\vdash f:\mathopen{}\left\langle{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}\Pi\,{u}}\mathrel{|}A\right\rangle\mathclose{}\end{array}\hfill}{\begin{array}[]{l l}{\mathbb{X}},u:{\mathbb{U}}\mathrel{|}\Gamma\vdash\mathsf{app}_{u}\cdot_{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}\Omega[{u}]}}f:A[{\scalebox{0.85}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faKey}}}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\mathsf{copy}_{u}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{app}_{u}}}]\end{array}\hfill}\text{}

are isomorphic:
\mathopen{}\left\llbracket{\mathbb{X}}\right\rrbracket\mathclose{}.(\Sigma(\mathbf{y}U).\mathopen{}\left\llbracket\Gamma\right\rrbracket\mathclose{})\cong(\mathopen{}\left\llbracket{\mathbb{X}}\right\rrbracket\mathclose{}\times\mathbf{y}U).\mathopen{}\left\llbracket\Gamma\right\rrbracket\mathclose{}
.

7.1.5.
Telescope quantification

Let
\Theta=(\Gamma,u:{\mathbb{U}},\delta:\Delta)
with no shape variables in
\Delta
.
Then
[\forall\,{u}]\Theta=(\Gamma,\forall\,{u}.(\delta:\Delta))
.
We translate this as follows:

\displaystyle\mathopen{}\left\{[\forall\,{u}]\Theta\right\}\mathclose{}

\displaystyle=\mathopen{}\left\{\Gamma,\forall\,{u}.(\delta:\Delta)\right\}\mathclose{}=\mathopen{}\left\{\Gamma\right\}\mathclose{}

\displaystyle\mathopen{}\left\langle[\forall\,{u}]\Theta\right\rangle\mathclose{}

\displaystyle=\mathopen{}\left\langle\Theta\right\rangle\mathclose{},\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}^{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\forall\,{u}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}{\between}[{u}]}}

\displaystyle(\ref{rule:ff:ctx-forall})

Let
\rho=(\sigma,u/u,\tau/\delta^{\prime}):\Theta=(\Gamma,u:{\mathbb{U}},\delta:\Delta)\to\Theta^{\prime}=(\Gamma^{\prime},u:{\mathbb{U}},\delta^{\prime}:\Delta^{\prime})
.
Then
[\forall({u/u})]\rho=(\sigma,{\mkern 0.75mu\mathchar 22\mkern-9.75mu\lambda}u.\tau/{\mkern 0.75mu\mathchar 22\mkern-9.75mu\lambda}u.\delta^{\prime})
.
We translate this as follows:

\displaystyle\mathopen{}\left\{[\forall({u/u})]\rho\right\}\mathclose{}

\displaystyle=\mathopen{}\left\{\sigma,{\mkern 0.75mu\mathchar 22\mkern-9.75mu\lambda}u.\tau/{\mkern 0.75mu\mathchar 22\mkern-9.75mu\lambda}u.\delta^{\prime}\right\}\mathclose{}=\mathopen{}\left\{\sigma\right\}\mathclose{}

\displaystyle\mathopen{}\left\langle[\forall({u/u})]\rho\right\rangle\mathclose{}

\displaystyle=\mathopen{}\left\langle\rho\right\rangle\mathclose{},\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}^{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\forall\,{u}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}{\between}[{u}]}}

\displaystyle(\ref{rule:ff:ctx-forall:fmap})

The rule
concerns
(\Gamma,\forall\,{u}.())=[\forall\,{u}](\Gamma,u:{\mathbb{U}})
which translates to
\mathopen{}\left\{\Gamma\right\}\mathclose{}\mathrel{|}\mathopen{}\left\langle\Gamma\right\rangle\mathclose{},\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}^{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\Finv[{u}]}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}\forall\,{u}}},\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}^{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\forall\,{u}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}{\between}[{u}]}}\,\mathsf{ctx}
, which is isomorphic to
\mathopen{}\left\{\Gamma\right\}\mathclose{}\mathrel{|}\mathopen{}\left\langle\Gamma\right\rangle\mathclose{}\,\mathsf{ctx}
by the 2-cell
(1_{\Gamma},{\scalebox{0.85}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faKey}}}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\mathsf{const}_{u}}:{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}1}\Rightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\forall\,{u}\circ\Finv[{u}]}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{unmer}_{u}}:{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}\forall\,{u}\circ{\between}[{u}]}\Rightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}1}})
because
{\mathbb{U}}
is
\top
-slice fully faithful (quantification

6.33
).
Naturality of

7.1.7.
Transpension type

Let
\Theta=(\Gamma,u:{\mathbb{U}},\delta:\Delta)
with no shape variables in
\Delta
.
The transpension type translates to a modal type:

\displaystyle\dfrac{\begin{array}[]{l l}\hskip-4.30554pt\text{\ref{rule:ff:transp}}\\
\mathopen{}\left\{\Gamma\right\}\mathclose{}\mathrel{|}\mathopen{}\left\langle\Theta\right\rangle\mathclose{},\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}^{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\forall\,{u}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}{\between}[{u}]}}\vdash\mathopen{}\left\langle A\right\rangle\mathclose{}\,\mathsf{type}\end{array}\hfill}{\begin{array}[]{l l}\mathopen{}\left\{\Gamma\right\}\mathclose{},u:{\mathbb{U}}\mathrel{|}\mathopen{}\left\langle\Theta\right\rangle\mathclose{}\vdash\mathopen{}\left\langle{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}{\between}[{u}]}\mathrel{|}\mathopen{}\left\langle A\right\rangle\mathclose{}\right\rangle\mathclose{}\,\mathsf{type}\end{array}\hfill}\text{}\qquad\dfrac{\begin{array}[]{l l}\hskip-4.30554pt\text{\ref{rule:ff:transp:intro}}\\
\mathopen{}\left\{\Gamma\right\}\mathclose{}\mathrel{|}\mathopen{}\left\langle\Theta\right\rangle\mathclose{},\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}^{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\forall\,{u}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}{\between}[{u}]}}\vdash\mathopen{}\left\langle a\right\rangle\mathclose{}:\mathopen{}\left\langle A\right\rangle\mathclose{}\end{array}\hfill}{\begin{array}[]{l l}\mathopen{}\left\{\Gamma\right\}\mathclose{},u:{\mathbb{U}}\mathrel{|}\mathopen{}\left\langle\Theta\right\rangle\mathclose{}\vdash\mathsf{mod}_{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}{\between}[{u}]}}\,a:\mathopen{}\left\langle{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}{\between}[{u}]}\mathrel{|}\mathopen{}\left\langle A\right\rangle\mathclose{}\right\rangle\mathclose{}\end{array}\hfill}\text{}

The eliminator is translated using
proposition

3.3
.

\displaystyle\dfrac{\begin{array}[]{l l}\hskip-4.30554pt\text{\ref{rule:ff:transp:elim}}\\
\mathopen{}\left\{\Gamma\right\}\mathclose{},u:{\mathbb{U}}\mathrel{|}\mathopen{}\left\langle\Gamma\right\rangle\mathclose{},\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}^{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\Finv[{u}]}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}\forall\,{u}}}\vdash t:\mathopen{}\left\langle{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}\forall\,{u}}\mathrel{|}\mathopen{}\left\langle A\right\rangle\mathclose{}[{\scalebox{0.85}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faKey}}}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\mathsf{const}_{u}^{-1}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{unmer}_{u}^{-1}}}]\right\rangle\mathclose{}\end{array}\hfill}{\begin{array}[]{l l}\mathopen{}\left\{\Gamma\right\}\mathclose{}\mathrel{|}\mathopen{}\left\langle\Gamma\right\rangle\mathclose{}\vdash\mathsf{unmer}_{u}\cdot_{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}\forall\,{u}}}t:\mathopen{}\left\langle A\right\rangle\mathclose{}\end{array}\hfill}\text{}

Again
{\scalebox{0.85}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faKey}}}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\mathsf{const}_{u}^{-1}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{unmer}_{u}^{-1}}}
just models
and can be ignored.
The
\beta
- and
\eta
-rules are the ones form
proposition

3.3
,
and the naturality rules amount to

\mathopen{}\left(\mathsf{mod}_{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}\forall\,{u}}}\,\mathopen{}\left\langle a\right\rangle\mathclose{}\right)\mathclose{}[\mathopen{}\left\langle\rho\right\rangle\mathclose{}]=\mathsf{mod}_{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}\forall\,{u}}}\,\mathopen{}\left(\mathopen{}\left\langle a\right\rangle\mathclose{}\mathopen{}\left[\mathopen{}\left\langle\rho\right\rangle\mathclose{},\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}^{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\forall\,{u}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}{\between}[{u}]}}\right]\mathclose{}\right)\mathclose{},\qquad\mathopen{}\left\langle{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}\forall\,{u}}\mathrel{|}\mathopen{}\left\langle A\right\rangle\mathclose{}\right\rangle\mathclose{}[\mathopen{}\left\langle\rho\right\rangle\mathclose{}]=\mathopen{}\left\langle{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}\forall\,{u}}\mathrel{|}\mathopen{}\left\langle A\right\rangle\mathclose{}\mathopen{}\left[\mathopen{}\left\langle\rho\right\rangle\mathclose{},\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}^{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\forall\,{u}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}{\between}[{u}]}}\right]\mathclose{}\right\rangle\mathclose{}.

This concludes the embedding for the selected typing rules in

.

7.2.
Internal transposition

In
section

2.3
, we proved the isomorphism

(\forall({u:{\mathbb{U}}}).A)\to B\quad\cong\quad\forall({u:{\mathbb{U}}}).(A\to{\between}[{u}]\,B).

(6)

The MTraS equivalent,

({\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}\forall\,{u}}\shortmid{A})\to B^{\prime}\quad\cong\quad\mathopen{}\left\langle{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}\forall\,{u}}\mathrel{|}A\to\mathopen{}\left\langle{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}{\between}[{u}]}\mathrel{|}B^{\prime}[{\scalebox{0.85}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faKey}}}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\mathsf{const}_{u}^{-1}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{unmer}_{u}^{-1}}}]\right\rangle\mathclose{}\right\rangle\mathclose{}

is not in general true (or even statable) but for
\top
-slice fully faithful multipliers it follows from the following instance of
proposition

3.4
(which holds in general)

\mathopen{}\left\langle{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}{\between}[{u}]}\mathrel{|}({\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}\forall\,{u}}\shortmid{A[{\scalebox{0.85}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faKey}}}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\mathsf{app}_{u}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{reidx}_{u}}}]})\to B\right\rangle\mathclose{}\quad\cong\quad\mathopen{}\left(A\to\mathopen{}\left\langle{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}{\between}[{u}]}\mathrel{|}B\right\rangle\mathclose{}\right)\mathclose{}

(7)

by applying
\mathopen{}\left\langle{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}\forall\,{u}}\mathrel{|}{\llcorner\hskip-1.72218pt\lrcorner}\right\rangle\mathclose{}
to both sides, redefining
B^{\prime}=B[{\scalebox{0.85}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faKey}}}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\mathsf{const}_{u}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{unmer}_{u}}}]
and using the quantification
theorem

6.33
.

7.3.
Higher-dimensional pattern matching

Deriving HDPM from internal transposition for general multipliers is a bit more involved than it was for FFTraS (
section

2.4
) because we have to use
eq.

7
instead of
eq.

6
.
However, we can construct an isomorphism
i:\mathopen{}\left\langle{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}\forall\,{u}}\mathrel{|}A\uplus B\right\rangle\mathclose{}\cong\mathopen{}\left\langle{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}\forall\,{u}}\mathrel{|}A\right\rangle\mathclose{}\uplus\mathopen{}\left\langle{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}\forall\,{u}}\mathrel{|}B\right\rangle\mathclose{}
directly by translating from
section

2.4
.
Again, the map to the left is trivial by pattern matching (which is still the original eliminator for modal types!).
The map to the right is given in either system by:

\displaystyle i:(\forall\,{u}.A\uplus B)\to(\forall\,{u}.A)\uplus(\forall\,{u}.B)\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\framebox{FFTraS}

\displaystyle i\,\hat{c}=\mathsf{unmer}\,\mathopen{}\left(u.\mathsf{case}\,\hat{c}\,u\,\mathsf{of}\mathopen{}\left\{\begin{array}[]{l c l}\mathsf{inl}\,a&\mapsto&\mathsf{mer}[{u}]\,(\mathsf{inl}\,({\mkern 0.75mu\mathchar 22\mkern-9.75mu\lambda}u.a))\\
\mathsf{inr}\,b&\mapsto&\mathsf{mer}[{u}]\,(\mathsf{inr}\,({\mkern 0.75mu\mathchar 22\mkern-9.75mu\lambda}u.b))\end{array}\right\}\mathclose{}\right)\mathclose{}

\displaystyle i:\mathopen{}\left\langle{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}\forall\,{u}}\mathrel{|}A\uplus B\right\rangle\mathclose{}\to\mathopen{}\left\langle{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}\forall\,{u}}\mathrel{|}A\right\rangle\mathclose{}\uplus\mathopen{}\left\langle{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}\forall\,{u}}\mathrel{|}B\right\rangle\mathclose{}\qquad\qquad\qquad\,\,\framebox{MTraS{}}

\displaystyle i\,\hat{c}=\mathsf{unmer}_{u}\cdot_{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}\forall\,{u}}}\mathsf{case}\,(\mathsf{app}_{u}\cdot_{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}\Finv[{u}]}}\hat{c}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\mathsf{drop}_{u}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{const}_{u}}})\,\mathsf{of}\mathopen{}\left\{\begin{array}[]{l c l}\mathsf{inl}\,a\mapsto\mathsf{mod}_{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}{\between}[{u}]}}\,(\mathsf{inl}\,(\mathsf{mod}_{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}\forall\,{u}}}\,(a^{{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\mathsf{app}_{u}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{reidx}_{u}}})))\\
\mathsf{inr}\,b\mapsto\mathsf{mod}_{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}{\between}[{u}]}}\,(\mathsf{inr}\,(\mathsf{mod}_{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}\forall\,{u}}}\,(b^{{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\mathsf{app}_{u}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{reidx}_{u}}})))\end{array}\right\}\mathclose{}.

8.
Additional Typing Rules

In this section, we add a few extensions to MTraS in order to reason about boundaries, and in order to recover all known presheaf operators in
section

10
.

8.1.
Subobject classifier

We add a universe of propositions (semantically the subobject classifier)
\mathsf{Prop}:\mathsf{U}_{0}
, with implicit encoding and decoding operations à la Coquand.
This universe is closed under logical operators and weak DRAs
[
Nuy20
, §6.5]
.
This is necessary to talk about
\Psi
and
\Phi
.
We identify all proofs of the same proposition.

8.2.
Boundary predicate

We add the following shape context constructor:

\dfrac{\begin{array}[]{l l}\hskip-4.30554pt\text{\hypertarget{rule:shp-ctx-boundary}{{shp-ctx-boundary}}}\\
{\mathbb{X}}\,\mathsf{shpctx}\qquad{\mathbb{U}}\,\mathsf{shape}\end{array}\hfill}{\begin{array}[]{l l}{\mathbb{X}},u:\partial{\mathbb{U}}\,\mathsf{shpctx}\end{array}\hfill}\text{\text{}}

modelling
\mathopen{}\left\llbracket{\mathbb{X}},u:\partial{\mathbb{U}}\right\rrbracket\mathclose{}=\mathopen{}\left\llbracket{\mathbb{X}}\right\rrbracket\mathclose{}\ltimes\partial U
(
definition

6.24
).
Write
(u\in\partial{\mathbb{U}})
for the presheaf morphism that includes
\mathopen{}\left\llbracket{\mathbb{X}},u:\partial{\mathbb{U}}\right\rrbracket\mathclose{}
in
\mathopen{}\left\llbracket{\mathbb{X}},u:{\mathbb{U}}\right\rrbracket\mathclose{}
.
We add a predicate of the same name
{\mathbb{X}},u:{\mathbb{U}}\mathrel{|}\cdot\vdash u\in\partial{\mathbb{U}}:\mathsf{Prop}
corresponding in the model to this subobject
\mathopen{}\left\llbracket{\mathbb{X}},u:\partial{\mathbb{U}}\right\rrbracket\mathclose{}\subseteq\mathopen{}\left\llbracket{\mathbb{X}},u:{\mathbb{U}}\right\rrbracket\mathclose{}
.
Note that, since the direct boundary was
not
defined by pullback, the boundary predicate is not preserved by shape substitution
\sigma:\mathopen{}\left\llbracket{\mathbb{X}}_{1}\right\rrbracket\mathclose{}\to\mathopen{}\left\llbracket{\mathbb{X}}_{2}\right\rrbracket\mathclose{}
, i.e.
\mathopen{}\left\langle{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}\Omega[{\sigma,u:=u}]}\mathrel{|}(u\in\partial{\mathbb{U}})_{{\mathbb{X}}_{2}}\right\rangle\mathclose{}
is not in general isomorphic to
(u\in\partial{\mathbb{U}})_{{\mathbb{X}}_{1}}
.

If we had modal type formers for
left
adjoints, then we could define the boundary predicate as
(\top,\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}^{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\Sigma({u\in\partial{\mathbb{U}}})}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}\Omega[{u\in\partial{\mathbb{U}}}]}})
.
However, MTT does not support such type formers and we do not know how to do this
18
18

18

It is worth noting that
\Sigma^{\sigma\mathrel{|}}
is a parametric right adjoint so the work by Gratzer et al.
[
GCK+22
]
could be relevant.

so we simply axiomatize the predicate by decreeing for every type
{\mathbb{X}},u:{\mathbb{U}}\mathrel{|}\Gamma\vdash A\,\mathsf{type}
an isomorphism

\displaystyle(u\in\partial{\mathbb{U}})\to A

\displaystyle\quad\cong\quad\mathopen{}\left\langle{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}\Pi({u\in\partial{\mathbb{U}}})}\mathrel{|}\mathopen{}\left\langle{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}\Omega[{u\in\partial{\mathbb{U}}}]}\mathrel{|}A[{\scalebox{0.85}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faKey}}}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\mathsf{drop}_{(u\in\partial{\mathbb{U}})}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{const}_{(u\in\partial{\mathbb{U}})}}}]\right\rangle\mathclose{}\right\rangle\mathclose{}.

(8)

In practice, for concrete systems, we will want axioms based on our findings in
section

6.3
, e.g. in a binary cubical system we would decree
{\cdot}\mathrel{|}(i\in\partial{\mathbb{I}})\leftrightarrow(i\equiv_{{\mathbb{I}}}0)\vee(i\equiv_{{\mathbb{I}}}1)
where the latter two predicates could be axiomatized similarly to (
8
).

8.3.
Strictness axiom

The strictness axiom
[
OP18
]
allows to extend a partial type
T
to a total type if
T
is isomorphic to a total type
A
, effectively strictifying the isomorphism:

\dfrac{\begin{array}[]{l l}{\mathbb{X}}\mathrel{|}\Gamma\vdash\varphi:\mathsf{Prop}\qquad{\mathbb{X}}\mathrel{|}\Gamma\vdash A:\mathsf{U}_{\ell}\qquad{\mathbb{X}}\mathrel{|}\Gamma,\_:\varphi\vdash T:\mathsf{U}_{\ell}\qquad{\mathbb{X}}\mathrel{|}\Gamma,\_:\varphi\vdash i:A\cong T\\
\end{array}\hfill}{\begin{array}[]{l l}{\mathbb{X}}\mathrel{|}\Gamma\vdash\mathsf{Strict}\mathopen{}\left\{A\cong\mathopen{}\left(\varphi\mathrel{?}T\mathrel{;}i\right)\mathclose{}\right\}\mathclose{}:\mathsf{U}_{\ell}\qquad{\mathbb{X}}\mathrel{|}\Gamma\vdash\mathsf{strict}\mathopen{}\left\{\varphi\mathrel{?}i\right\}\mathclose{}:A\cong\mathsf{Strict}\mathopen{}\left\{A\cong\mathopen{}\left(\varphi\mathrel{?}T\mathrel{;}i\right)\mathclose{}\right\}\mathclose{}\\
\mathrm{where}\,\,\Gamma,\_:\varphi\vdash\mathsf{Strict}\mathopen{}\left\{A\cong\mathopen{}\left(\varphi\mathrel{?}T\mathrel{;}i\right)\mathclose{}\right\}\mathclose{}=T:\mathsf{U}_{\ell}\qquad\Gamma,\_:\varphi\vdash\mathsf{strict}\mathopen{}\left\{\varphi\mathrel{?}i\right\}\mathclose{}=i:A\cong T\end{array}\hfill}\text{}

9.
Investigating the Transpension Type

In
section

2.2
, we have briefly investigated the structure of a fully faithful transpension type in FFTraS.
In this section, we investigate the structure of the general transpension type
\mathopen{}\left\langle{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}{\between}[{u}]}\mathrel{|}A\right\rangle\mathclose{}
in MTraS.

9.1.
Poles

Our first observation is that on the boundary, the transpension type is trivial.
Let
{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}\top}:{\mathbb{X}}_{1}\to{\mathbb{X}}_{2}
be the modality, between any two modes, which maps any presheaf to the terminal presheaf.
We clearly have
{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}\top\circ\mu}={\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}\top}
for any
{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}\mu}
, but also
{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}\mu\circ\top}\cong{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}\top}
because all internal modalities are right adjoints and therefore preserve the terminal object.

Theorem 9.1

(Pole)
.

We have
{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}\Omega[{u\in\partial{\mathbb{U}}}]\circ{\between}[{u}]}\cong{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}\top}
.
We can thus postulate a term
{\mathbb{X}},u:{\mathbb{U}}\mathrel{|}\Gamma,\_:u\in\partial{\mathbb{U}}\vdash\mathsf{pole}:\mathopen{}\left\langle{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}{\between}[{u}]}\mathrel{|}T\right\rangle\mathclose{}
for any
{\mathbb{X}}\mathrel{|}\Gamma,\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}{\between}[{u}]}}\vdash T\,\mathsf{type}
, with an
\eta
-rule
{\mathbb{X}},u:{\mathbb{U}}\mathrel{|}\Gamma,\_:u\in\partial{\mathbb{U}}\vdash t=\mathsf{pole}:\mathopen{}\left\langle{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}{\between}[{u}]}\mathrel{|}T\right\rangle\mathclose{}
.

Proof 9.2

(Sketch of proof)
.

The left adjoints
{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\forall({u:{\mathbb{U}}})\circ\Sigma({u\in\partial{\mathbb{U}}})}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\bot}
of the concerned modalities are isomorphic because
\forall({u:{\mathbb{U}}}).(u\in\partial{\mathbb{U}})
is false.
We give a full proof in the technical report
[
Nuy23b
]
.

Definition

6.8
of the boundary relied on the notion of dimensional splitness.
The following result shows that it was a good one: the transpension is
only
trivial on the boundary:

Theorem 9.3

(Boundary)
.

In the model, we have
[
Nuy23b
]

{\mathbb{X}},u:{\mathbb{U}}\mathrel{|}\Gamma\vdash(u\in\partial{\mathbb{U}})\cong\mathopen{}\left\langle{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}{\between}[{u:{\mathbb{U}}}]}\mathrel{|}{\mathsf{Empty}}\right\rangle\mathclose{}.

9.2.
Meridians

As all our modalities are proper DRAs
[
BCM+20
]
, the modal introduction rule is invertible in the model.
This immediately shows that sections
19
19

19

By a section of a dependent type, we mean a dependent function with the same domain as the type.

of the transpension type

\displaystyle{\mathbb{X}}\mathrel{|}\Gamma\vdash f:\mathopen{}\left\langle{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}\forall({u:{\mathbb{U}}})}\mathrel{|}\mathopen{}\left\langle{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}{\between}[{u:{\mathbb{U}}}]}\mathrel{|}T\right\rangle\mathclose{}\right\rangle\mathclose{}

(which we call meridians) are in 1-1 correspondence with terms

\displaystyle{\mathbb{X}}\mathrel{|}\Gamma,\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}^{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\forall\,{u}\circ\Finv[{u}]}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}\forall\,{u}\circ{\between}[{u}]}}\vdash t:T.

If it were not for the locking of the context, this characterization in terms of poles and meridians would make the transpension type look quite similar to a dependent version of the suspension type in HoTT
[
Uni13
]
, whence our choice of name.
If
{\mathbb{U}}
is
\top
-slice (hence presheafwise) fully faithful, then the applied locks are actually isomorphic to the identity lock (
theorem

6.33
).
In any case, regardless of the properties of
{\mathbb{U}}
,
proposition

3.3
tells us that the
\mathsf{let}
-rule for
{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}{\between}[{u:{\mathbb{U}}}]}
has the same power as

\displaystyle\mathsf{unmer}_{u}

\displaystyle:({\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}\forall({u:{\mathbb{U}}})}\shortmid{\mathopen{}\left\langle{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}{\between}[{u:{\mathbb{U}}}]}\mathrel{|}T\right\rangle\mathclose{}})\to T[{\scalebox{0.85}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faKey}}}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\mathsf{const}_{u}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{unmer}_{u}}}]

which extracts meridians.
If
{\mathbb{U}}
is
\top
-slice fully faithful, then the 2-cell
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{unmer}_{u}}
is invertible (
theorem

6.33
) and we can also straightforwardly
create
meridians from elements of
T[{\scalebox{0.85}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faKey}}}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\mathsf{const}_{u}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{unmer}_{u}}}]
.

9.3.
Pattern matching

The eliminator
\mathsf{unmer}_{u}
is only capable of eliminating
sections
of the transpension type.
If the quotient
theorem

6.30
applies to
{\mathbb{U}}
, we can eliminate locally by pattern matching:

\dfrac{\begin{array}[]{l l}\hskip-4.30554pt\text{\hypertarget{rule:transp:elim}{{transp:elim}}}\\
{\mathbb{X}},u:{\mathbb{U}}\mathrel{|}\Gamma\,\mathsf{ctx}\\
{\mathbb{X}}\mathrel{|}\Gamma,\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}^{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\forall\,{u}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}{\between}[{u}]}}\vdash A\,\mathsf{type}\\
{\mathbb{X}},u:{\mathbb{U}}\mathrel{|}\Gamma,r:\mathopen{}\left\langle{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}{\between}[{u}]}\mathrel{|}A\right\rangle\mathclose{}\vdash C\,\mathsf{type}\\
{\mathbb{X}},u:{\mathbb{U}}\mathrel{|}\Gamma,\_:u\in\partial{\mathbb{U}}\vdash c_{\mathsf{pole}}:C[\mathsf{pole}/r]\\
{\mathbb{X}},u:{\mathbb{U}}\mathrel{|}\Gamma,\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}^{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\forall\,{u}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}{\between}[{u}]}},x:A,\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}^{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\Finv[{u}]}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}\forall\,{u}}}\vdash c_{\mathsf{mer}}:C\mathopen{}\left[{\mathrm{id}}_{\Gamma},{\scalebox{0.85}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faKey}}}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\mathsf{app}_{u}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{reidx}_{u}}},\mathsf{mod}_{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}{\between}[{u}]}}\,(x^{{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\mathsf{const}_{u}^{-1}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{unmer}_{u}^{-1}}})/r\right]\mathclose{}\\
{\mathbb{X}},u:{\mathbb{U}}\mathrel{|}\Gamma,\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}^{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\forall\,{u}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}{\between}[{u}]}},x:A,\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}^{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\Finv[{u}]}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}\forall\,{u}}},\_:u\in\partial{\mathbb{U}}\vdash c_{\mathsf{mer}}=c_{\mathsf{pole}}[{\mathrm{id}}_{\Gamma},{\scalebox{0.85}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faKey}}}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\mathsf{app}_{u}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{reidx}_{u}}}]:C[{\mathrm{id}}_{\Gamma},{\scalebox{0.85}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faKey}}}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\mathsf{app}_{u}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{reidx}_{u}}},\mathsf{pole}/r]\\
{\mathbb{X}},u:{\mathbb{U}}\mathrel{|}\Gamma\vdash t:\mathopen{}\left\langle{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}{\between}[{u}]}\mathrel{|}A\right\rangle\mathclose{}\end{array}\hfill}{\begin{array}[]{l l}{\mathbb{X}},u:{\mathbb{U}}\mathrel{|}\Gamma\vdash c:=\mathsf{case}\,t\,\mathsf{of}\mathopen{}\left\{\begin{array}[]{l c l}\mathsf{pole}\mapsto c_{\mathsf{pole}}\mathrel{|}\mathsf{mer}\,x\mapsto c_{\mathsf{mer}}\end{array}\right\}\mathclose{}:C[t/r]\\
\mathrm{where}\,\,c[\mathsf{pole}/t]=c_{\mathsf{pole}}\\
\phantom{\mathrm{where}}\,\,{\mathbb{X}},u:{\mathbb{U}}\mathrel{|}\Delta,\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}^{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\Finv[{u}]}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}\forall\,{u}}}\vdash c=c_{\mathsf{mer}}[{\mathrm{id}}_{\Delta},{\scalebox{0.85}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faKey}}}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\mathsf{const}_{u}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\mathsf{unmer}_{u}}},\mathsf{unmer}_{u}\cdot_{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}\forall\,{u}}}t/x,\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}^{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\Finv[{u}]}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}\forall\,{u}}}]:C\end{array}\hfill}\text{\text{}}

Figure 8.
Transpension elimination by pattern matching (sound if
{\mathbb{U}}
is
\top
-slice fully faithful and shard-free). Recall
eq.

1
.

Theorem 9.4
.

If
{\mathbb{U}}
is
\top-slice
(hence presheafwise) fully faithful and shard-free, then the rule
in
fig.

8
is sound
[
Nuy23b
]
.

The elimination rule is best understood by looking at the left names.
We get a context
\Gamma
depending on
u:{\mathbb{U}}
, a type
A
depending on sections of
\Gamma
(as represented by
\Gamma,\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}^{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\forall\,{u}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}{\between}[{u}]}}
), a type
C
depending on
u
and
r:\mathopen{}\left\langle{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}{\between}[{u}]}\mathrel{|}A\right\rangle\mathclose{}
, and an argument
t
of type
\mathopen{}\left\langle{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}{\between}[{u}]}\mathrel{|}A\right\rangle\mathclose{}
.
To obtain a value of type
C
, we need to give an action
c_{\mathsf{pole}}
on the boundary, where
t
is necessarily
\mathsf{pole}
(pole
theorem

9.1
), and a compatible action on sections of the transpension type, i.e. meridians, which live over sections of
\Gamma
but are themselves essentially elements of
A
(quantification
theorem

6.33
), producing sections of
C
(but the quantifier
{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}\forall\,{u}}
has been brought to the left as
\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}^{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\Finv[{u}]}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}\forall\,{u}}}
).
Thanks to shard-freedom, we know that everything that is not a section
20
20

20

or a ‘dimensional section’ in case of base categories that are not objectwise pointable
, is on the boundary, so this suffices.

The computation rule for meridians fires when all of
\Gamma
is fresh for
u
. In this situation, the judgement for
t
is
{\mathbb{X}},u:{\mathbb{U}}\mathrel{|}\Delta,\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}^{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0}\Finv[{u}]}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}\forall\,{u}}}\vdash t:\mathopen{}\left\langle{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}{\between}[{u}]}\mathrel{|}A\right\rangle\mathclose{}
which by transposition boils down to
{\mathbb{X}}\mathrel{|}\Delta\vdash t^{\prime}:\mathopen{}\left\langle{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}\forall\,{a}}\mathrel{|}\mathopen{}\left\langle{\color[rgb]{0.6,0.3,0}{\between}[{u}]}\mathrel{|}A\right\rangle\mathclose{}\right\rangle\mathclose{}
, i.e. it fires when
t
can actually be seen as a full section of the transpension type, so that we can apply the action on sections given by
c_{\mathsf{mer}}
.
This computation rule is in a non-general context and needs to be forcibly closed under substitution (we have not found a better way to phrase this computation rule, but neither do we exclude that there exists one).
